Title exactly: "A BODY IN THE LIGHT". Subtitle: "Point One · final text".
Panel 1: Caption exactly "The name arrives before its object.". Scene: An empty place over a plain pale plinth in a corridor, labelled Point one. Student leans closer with an open hand in empty space. Absolutely no hidden sphere, outline or ghost at this station.
Panel 2: Caption exactly "You have crossed something you could see.". Scene: Next station: a small white sphere hangs over its label Point one. Student leans past its surface; use a separate clean little cutaway inset to suggest the view crossing without putting a disembodied hand inside a ball.
Panel 3: Caption exactly "The difference was invisible until you tried to cross it.". Scene: A second SAME-sized white sphere, visually identical. Student's approach stops before its surface. Dotted explanatory boundary inset, delicate and separate; no force-field effect.
Panel 4: Caption exactly "You have removed their sun.". Scene: Student turns a plain wall switch. Matched scene with the same spheres, long crooked shadows disappear. Objects remain faintly lit by ambient light, never vanish. A small on/off paired inset is allowed.
Panel 5: Caption exactly "Its boundary has not gone with its shadow.". Scene: Student approaches the same solid white sphere in the dimmer room. The object remains visibly present and its boundary remains. Older companion looks, no teaching speech.
Panel 6: Caption exactly "The same short address leaves a great deal unsaid.". Scene: A much larger white sphere on a plinth, labelled Point one, around 1.3m across. Adult student must walk around it; it occludes more of the room, not the size of a handball. Light back on, long shadow.
Footer exactly: "We have given each a body and called each a point.".

Scene: Exactly SEVEN small choir specimens on risers: plane, box, sphere, cylinder, capsule, prism, torus, gently posed in separate places. Nearby letter and TWO resting trail-strip specimens lie outside the choir, showing it is a selection. No singing faces or humanoid shapes.
Footer exactly: "Which inherited choice would you keep, and which would you want to make yourself?".

Panel 3: Caption exactly "What looked like an address on a diagram has become a place where your body can drop.". A little ordered comparison has concentric aligned frames around SAME dot. Visitor shifts sideways; intersections separate in depth. Panel 6: Caption exactly "Their transparency lets us see through the structure without promising that we can read it.". Dense transparent frames in four layers, not an opaque cube block. Visitor looks for a site among lines; companion watches rather than lectures. Do not assert each crossing is a shared vertex.

Panel 1: Caption exactly "A figure crosses the joints. No larger tile has been added.". Scene: Close floor-study desk: EXACTLY TWO square 4-by-4 samples, both using same unit square bisected diagonally black/white. BEFORE all sixteen triangles have the SAME orientation. AFTER address-dependent quarter-turns produce a joined figure across neighboring tile seams. Use Image2's two samples as geometry reference, not its dark rendering. Visitor presses APPLY/UNDO. No whole black/white checker cells substituted for bisected motifs.
Panel 2: Caption exactly "The instruction has not grown longer. It is being asked again at more places.". Scene: Visitor and companion beside larger 16-by-16 flat motif field; restrained orange outline encloses exactly a 4-by-4 corner sample within 256 addresses. A short diagram card repeats rule (cx+cy)%4. Pattern, not floor height, changes. Main image high oblique lets small patch and larger field be read as SAME unit at more sites.
Panel 3: Caption exactly "A local edit and a rule repeated everywhere need not have the same reach.". Scene: Visitor uses NEXT TILE. One outlined tile [1,0] receives 1 quarter-turn; nearby [2,0] receives 2 quarter-turns in explanatory inset. Other tiles remain identifiable. No enlarged unique master shape. Modulo chooses a turn count; zero is an orientation, not an empty cell.
Panel 4: Caption exactly "One changed source can have different consequences under different rules.". Scene: Two comparison fields on flat floor, labelled TRANSLATE and REFLECT. A paired inset shows TURN MOTIF rotates the SAME diagonal source tile 90 degrees while their different placement/reflection rules remain. Visitor looks between fields. Reflection switches handedness in alternate columns, translation keeps orientation repeated. Never depict a moved wall or lifted tile here.
Panel 5: Caption exactly "The same square is still here, turned once more than its neighbours.". Scene: One repeated translation patch with ONE EXCEPTION switched on: one clearly outlined bisected cell rotated by an extra quarter-turn; all neighboring tile orientations are identical and unchanged. Show an off/on comparison of the same patch; ONLY the selected cell differs. No new tile shape, hole or raised relief.
Panel 6: Caption exactly "Your eye can find a ridge where your foot finds no step.". Scene: Low view of adult foot crossing a black-and-white pattern joint on COMPLETELY LEVEL floor. Strong optical chevrons appear sculptural, but foot rests on one continuous plane, with no raised triangles and no black voids. Companion sees visitor walk across freely. The printed colours cannot change collision.
Footer exactly: "The unit that you notice need not be the unit stored by the program.".

Companion visible INSIDE hall. Small landing bounded by rail, no floor extending beyond it, no implied next immense room outside. Seeing plain back reveals how little surface enclosed the earlier walk. Whole wall stationary open while visitor stays outside.
Footer exactly: "The first changed what we could read; the second changed where we could go.".

Distinguish selected persistent event dots from transient highlight. Start from a blank small inset; no piano keys instead of grid.
Panel 5: Caption exactly "The two clocks are separate, but the sound can still leave a visible response.". Scene: Floor console on HOLD: settled checker with one brightened column. Sequencer still plays, its highlight advances. Small paired view has sequencer stopped while floor continues animation; label FLOOR PLAY / SCORE STOP. Avoid a single master switch stopping both. Two separate circular clock overlays may explain timing, no physical clock objects invented.
Panel 6: Caption exactly "The sequencer tells the addresses apart; that flash alone cannot.". Scene: Clean small comparison showing sixteen sequencer steps and TWELVE floor columns. Activate only step0, then only step12 in second-track paired moments. Both brighten floor column0. Diagram labels 0%12=0 and 12%12=0, same floor-column highlight, different score indices. Visitor walks nearby; no footprint sensors or score events written by feet.
Footer exactly: "Sharing a space with a rhythm is different from being measured by it.".

Panel 4: Caption exactly "That knowledge has not entered the algorithm merely because it has entered your view.". Scene: Visitor walks to side of table and sees the lower valley beyond a ridge; gold point stays in other higher basin. Viewer line of sight indicated gently, no trail hopping ridge, no claim seeing steers marker. Museum bodies and table prominent rather than large extra dashboard.
Panel 5: Caption exactly "These endings answer different questions.". Scene: Three compact SCHEMATIC condition cards near existing console: SMALL SLOPE local arrows short; LEFT FRAME proposed dashed step goes beyond border while last valid gold point remains INSIDE; STEP LIMIT finite trail ends with a budget icon. Clearly distinct stopping rules, no endpoint declared global solution.
Panel 6: Caption exactly "The height makes cost legible across a table.". Scene: Wide quiet view adult and older companion beside blue surface above table. Small height relation card 'display height = 0.40 + 0.22 × cost'. Height explains display, not gravitational energy or a walkable terrain. Only established existing artifacts in background; no human travelling on mesh.
Footer exactly "Nothing in the arithmetic establishes that this measure contains everything worth keeping.".

Adult visitor and older companion look up, no hand plucking or motion streaks. Modelled mass plaques attached to discs, symbolic m labels rather than fabricated measured grams. Panel 5: Caption exactly "This construction has not measured either quantity.". Caption board RECURSIVE BRACED STOREYS / NO LOAD TEST. Sway marks only whole prescribed pose, no simulated wind or collapse. No tower described as optimum.
Panel 6: Caption exactly "Before asking whether a form has reached the bottom, ask what was allowed to move and what counted as success.". Scene: Adult and companion between static mobile, triangle and tower, quiet room-level view. Separate small labels ENCODED BALANCE / ANIMATED POSE / UNTESTED LOAD. No new physical experiment performed; objects keep established dispositions. Let art stay beautiful while differences become inspectable.
Footer exactly "A picture of structure is not yet an instrument for its strength.".

Title exactly "A MOVEMENT KEPT IN A COLUMN". Subtitle "Bernini · final text".
Panel 1: Caption exactly "Can a shape keep the rhythm of a movement without travelling along it?". Scene: Adult and companion enter high warm-marble twisting colonnade, repeated sculptural shafts. Sober pale museum with dramatic calm light, no literal Vatican/history reenactment..
Panel 2: Caption exactly "Choose one ridge on a twisting shaft and follow it upward.". Scene: Adult gazes up following one subtle highlighted helical ridge, arrow HEIGHT upward. One continuous twisted column fixed central axis, human on floor..
Panel 3: Caption exactly "This second observation follows change through time.". Scene: Two schematic times at SAME HEIGHT: column cross-section rotates as whole, TIME arrow, not column stretching or ridge travelling vertically..
Panel 4: Caption exactly "The form and its arrangement contribute different things: one supplies the twist, the other decides whether you meet a field of objects, a threshold or an aisle.". Scene: Wide repeated current colonnade view with sightline through gaps. Small conceptual silhouettes FIELD / THRESHOLD / AISLE explicitly possibilities, no arrangement control or claim this version switches..
Panel 5: Caption exactly "Touching a shaft does not play it.". Scene: Hand resting near shaft, separate low HUM label with ROTATION SPEED relation. No magical touch sparks, no playable vibrating notes caused by finger..
Panel 6: Caption exactly "Which “now” has the repeated phrase brought back?". Scene: Adult pauses by separate wall voice instrument labelled 'right here, right now', column keeps rotating in background. Small repeat arrow around phrase, not words spoken by column or protagonist..
Footer exactly "Watching that rotation does not explain how the twist was made, and following the twist does not require the column to rotate.".
